For  $i \neq m$ , we get that  $\frac{\partial Q_v^c(x)}{\partial x_i} = \frac{1}{t_i - s_i} \frac{\partial Q_v^c(x)}{\partial p_i} = \frac{1}{t_i' - s_i'} \frac{\partial Q_v^{c'}(x)}{\partial p_i'} = \frac{\partial Q_v^{c'}(x)}{\partial x_i}$  since  $\overline{t_i = t_i'}$  and  $s_i = s_i'$  for all  $i \neq m$ . The latter argument cannot be applied for the  $m$ -th coordinate since  $t_m - s_m = -(t_m' - s_m')$ . However since  $x$  belongs to the boundary of both the cubelets  $L(c)$  and  $L(c')$  it is implied that  $p_m = p_m'$  is either 0 or 1, meaning that  $\frac{\partial Q_v^c(x)}{\partial x_m} = \frac{\partial Q_v^{c'}(x)}{\partial x_m} = 0$  since  $S'(0) = S'(1) = 0$  from Lemma 8.3.  
(c)  $\frac{\partial^2 Q_v^c(x)}{\partial x_i \partial x_j} = \frac{\partial^2 Q_v^c'(x)}{\partial x_i \partial x_j}$ . For  $i, j \neq m$ , we get that  $\frac{\partial^2 Q_v^c(x)}{\partial x_i \partial x_j} = \frac{1}{t_i - s_i} \frac{1}{t_j - s_j} \frac{\partial^2 Q_v^c(x)}{\partial p_i \partial p_j} = \frac{1}{t_i' - s_i'} \frac{1}{t_j' - s_j'} \frac{\partial Q_v^c'(x)}{\partial p_i' \partial p_j'} = \frac{\partial^2 Q_v^c'(x)}{\partial x_i \partial x_j}$  since  $t_i = t_i'$  and  $s_i = s_i'$  for all  $i \neq m$ . As in the previous case,  $p_m = p_m'$  equals either 0 or 1. As a result,  $\frac{\partial^2 Q_v^c(x)}{\partial x_m \partial x_j} = \frac{\partial^2 Q_v^c'(x)}{\partial x_m \partial x_j} = 0$  since  $S'(0) = S'(1) = S''(0) = S''(1) = 0$  by Lemma 8.3.

2. Since  $v \in L_c(c) \setminus L_c(c')$ , we get that  $v_m = c_m$ . In case  $c_m$  is even, we get that  $s_m = c_m = v_m$  and thus the coordinate the coordinate  $m$  belongs in the set  $A_v^c$ . Since  $x$  coincides with one of the corners in  $L_c(c) \setminus L_c(c')$  we get that  $p_m = 1$  which combined with the fact that  $m \in A_v^c$  implies that  $Q_v^c(x) = 0$  (see Definition 8.7). Then by Lemma C.1,  $\frac{\partial Q_v^{c'}(x)}{\partial x_i} = \frac{\partial^2 Q_v^{c'}(x)}{\partial x_i \partial x_j} = 0$ . In case is odd, we get that  $s_m = c_m + 1$ . The latter combined with the fact that  $v_m = c_m$  implies that the  $m$ -th coordinate belongs in  $B_v^c$ . Now  $p_m = 0$  and by Definition 8.7,  $Q_v^c(x) = 0$ . Then again by Lemma C.1,  $\frac{\partial Q_v^{c'}(x)}{\partial x_i} = \frac{\partial^2 Q_v^{c'}(x)}{\partial x_i \partial x_j} = 0$ .  
3. This case follows with the same reasoning with previous case 2.

We are now ready to prove Lemma C.2.

Proof of Lemma C.2. 1. Let  $\pmb{v} \in L_{c}(\pmb{c}) \cap L_{c}(\pmb{c}^{\prime})$ . There exists a sequence of corners

$$
\boldsymbol {c} = \boldsymbol {c} ^ {(1)}, \dots , \boldsymbol {c} ^ {(m)} = \boldsymbol {c} ^ {\prime}
$$

such that  $\left\| \pmb{c}^{(j)} - \pmb{c}^{(j + 1)}\right\|_{1} = 1$  and  $\pmb {v}\in L_c(\pmb {c}^j)$  for all  $j\in [m]$ . By Lemma C.4 we get that,

(a)  $Q_{v}^{c(j)}(x) = Q_{v}^{c(j + 1)}(x).$  
(b)  $\frac{\partial Q_v^{c(j)}(x)}{\partial x_i} = \frac{\partial Q_v^{c(j + 1)}(x)}{\partial x_i}$

(c)  $\frac{\partial^2Q_v^{c(j)}(x)}{\partial x_i\partial x_j} = \frac{\partial Q_v^{c(j + 1)}(x)}{\partial x_i\partial x_j}.$

which implies Case 1 of Lemma C.2.

2. Let  $\pmb{v} \in L_{c}(\pmb{c}) \setminus L_{c}(\pmb{c}')$ . There exists a sequence of corners  $\pmb{c} = \pmb{c}^{(1)} \ldots, \pmb{c}^{(i)}$  such that  $\left\| \pmb{c}^{(j)} - \pmb{c}^{(j + 1)} \right\|_{1} = 1$  and  $\pmb{v} \notin L_{c} \pmb{c}^{(i)}$  and  $\pmb{v} \in L_{c}(\pmb{c}^{(j)})$  for all  $j < i$ . By case 2 of Lemma C.4 we get that  $Q_{v}^{c(i - 1)}(\pmb{x}) = \frac{\partial Q_{v}^{c(i - 1)}(\pmb{x})}{\partial x_{i}} = \frac{\partial^{2} Q_{v}^{c(i - 1)}(\pmb{x})}{\partial x_{i} \partial x_{j}} = 0$ . Then case 2 of Lemma C.2 follows by case 1 of Lemma C.4.  
3. Similarly with case 2.

# C.2 Proof of Lemma 8.11

We start this section with some fundamental properties of the smooth step function  $S_{\infty}$  that are more fine-grained than the properties we presented in Lemma 8.3.

Lemma C.5. For  $d \geq 10$  there exists a universal constant  $c > 0$  such that the following statements hold.

1. If  $x \geq 1 / d$  then  $S_{\infty}(x) \geq c \cdot 2^{-d}$ .  
2. If  $x \leq 1 / d$  then  $S_{\infty}'(x) \leq c \cdot d^2 \cdot 2^{-d}$ .  
3. If  $x \geq 1 / d$  then  $\frac{S_{\infty}^{\prime}(x)}{S_{\infty}(x)} \leq c \cdot d^{2}$ .  
4. If  $x \leq 1 / d$  then  $|S_{\infty}^{\prime \prime}(x)| \leq c \cdot d^{4} \cdot 2^{-d}$ .  
5. If  $x \geq 1 / d$  then  $\frac{|S_{\infty}^{\prime\prime}(x)|}{S_{\infty}(x)} \leq c \cdot d^{4}$ .

Proof. We compute the derivative of  $S_{\infty}$  and we have that

$$
S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime} (x) = \ln (2) S _ {\infty} (x) S _ {\infty} (1 - x) \left(\frac {1}{x ^ {2}} + \frac {1}{(1 - x) ^ {2}}\right)
$$

from which we immediately get  $S_{\infty}'(x) \geq 0$ . Then we can compute the second derivative of  $S_{\infty}$  as follows

$$
\begin{array}{l} S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime \prime} (x) = \ln (2) S _ {\infty} (x) S _ {\infty} (1 - x). \\ \cdot \left(\ln (2) \left(S _ {\infty} (1 - x) - S _ {\infty} (x)\right) \left(\frac {1}{x ^ {2}} + \frac {1}{(1 - x) ^ {2}}\right) ^ {2} - 2 \left(\frac {1}{x ^ {3}} - \frac {1}{(1 - x) ^ {3}}\right)\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

We next want to prove that  $S_{\infty}''(x) \geq 0$  for  $x \leq 1/10$ . To see this observe that  $1 - 2 \cdot S_{\infty}(x) \geq 1/2$  for  $x \leq 1/d$  and therefore

$$
S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime \prime} (x) \geq \frac {\ln (2)}{x ^ {3}} S _ {\infty} (x) S _ {\infty} (1 - x) \left(\frac {\ln (2)}{2 x} - 2\right)
$$

hence for  $x \leq 4 / \ln(2)$  it holds that  $S_{\infty}''(x) \geq 0$ . By similar but more tedious calculations we can conclude that  $S_{\infty}'''(x) \geq 0$  for  $x \leq 1/10$ . Hence in the interval  $x \in [0,1/10]$  all the functions  $S_{\infty}, S_{\infty}', S_{\infty}''$  are all increasing functions of  $x$ .

Next we show that the function  $h(x) = 2^{-1/x} + 2^{-1/(1-x)}$  is upper and lower bounded. First observe that  $h(x) \geq \max\{2^{-1/x}, 2^{-1/(1-x)}\}$ . Now if we set  $t(x) = 2^{-1/x}$  then  $t'(x) = \ln(2)t(x)/x^2$  and hence  $t(x) \geq t(1/2) = 1/4$  for  $x \geq 1/2$ . The same way we can prove that  $2^{-1/(1-x)} \geq 1/4$  for  $x \leq 1/2$ . Therefore  $h(x) \geq 1/4$  for all  $x \in [0,1]$ . Also it is not hard to see that  $2^{-1/x} \leq 1/2$  and  $2^{-1/(1-x)} \leq 1/2$  which implies  $h(x) \leq 1$ . Hence overall we have that  $h(x) \in [1/4,1]$  for all  $x \in [0,1]$ . We are now ready to prove the statements.

1. We have shown that  $S_{\infty}'(x) \geq 0$  for all  $x \in [0,1]$ . Hence  $S_{\infty}$  is an increasing function and therefore  $S_{\infty}(x) \geq S_{\infty}(1 / d)$  for  $x \geq 1 / d$ . Now we have that  $S_{\infty}(1 / d) = 2^{-d} / h(1 / d) \geq 2^{-d}$ .  
2. Since  $S_{\infty}'(x)$  is increasing for  $x \in [0,1/10]$ , we have that  $S_{\infty}'(x) \leq S_{\infty}'(1/d)$  for  $x \leq 1/d$  and therefore

$$
\begin{array}{l} S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime} (x) \leq \ln (2) S _ {\infty} (1 - 1 / d) S _ {\infty} (1 / d) \left(d ^ {2} + \frac {1}{\left(1 - \frac {1}{d}\right) ^ {2}}\right) \\ \leq 2 \ln (2) \frac {2 ^ {- d}}{h (1 / d)} \leq 8 \ln (2) 2 ^ {- d}. \\ \end{array}
$$

3. We have that for  $x \leq 1 / d$

$$
\frac {S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime} (x)}{S _ {\infty} (x)} = \ln (2) S _ {\infty} (1 - x) \left(\frac {1}{x ^ {2}} + \frac {1}{(1 - x) ^ {2}}\right) \leq 2 \ln (2) \frac {1}{x ^ {2}} \leq 2 \ln (2) d ^ {2}.
$$

4. Follows directly from the statement 1., the fact that  $S_{\infty}^{\prime \prime}(x)$  is increasing for  $x \in [0,1 / 10]$  and the above expression of  $S_{\infty}^{\prime \prime}$  this statement follows.  
5. This statement follows using the same reasoning with statement 3.

In this section we establish the bounds on the gradient and the hessian of  $\mathrm{P}_v(x)$ . These bounds are formally stated in Lemma 8.11 the proof of which is the main goal of the section.

Lemma 8.11. For any vertex  $\pmb{v} \in ([N] - 1)^d$ , it holds that

1.  $\left|\frac{\partial\mathrm{P}_v(x)}{\partial x_i}\right|\leq \Theta (d^{12} / \delta),$  
2.  $\left|\frac{\partial^2\mathrm{P}_v(x)}{\partial x_i\partial x_j}\right|\leq \Theta (d^{24} / \delta^2).$

In order to prove Lemma 8.11. We first introduce several technical lemmas.

Lemma C.6. Let  $\pmb{x} \in [0,1]^d$  lying in cublet  $L(\pmb{c})$ , with  $\pmb{c} \in ([N] - 1)^d$  and let  $\pmb{p}_x^c = (p_1, \dots, p_d)$  be the canonical representation of  $\pmb{x}$ . Then for all vertices  $\pmb{v} \in L_c(\pmb{c})$ , it holds that

$$
\left| \frac {\partial Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right| \leq \Theta (d ^ {1 1}) \cdot \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} \in V _ {c}} Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x}).
$$

Proof. To simplify notation we use  $Q_{v}(x)$  instead of  $Q_{v}^{c}(x)$ ,  $A$  instead of  $A_{v}^{c}$  and  $B$  instead of  $B_{v}^{c}$  for the rest of the proof. Without loss of generality, we assume that for all  $j \in A$  and  $\ell \in B$ ,  $p_{\ell} > p_{j}$  since otherwise  $\frac{\partial Q_{v}^{c}(x)}{\partial p_{i}} = 0$  trivially by the Definition 8.7. Let  $i \in B$  (symmetrically for  $i \in A$ ) then,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {\partial Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right| = \\ = \prod_ {\ell \neq i} \prod_ {j \in A} S _ {\infty} (S (p _ {\ell}) - S (p _ {j})) \cdot \left[ \sum_ {j \in A} \left| S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime} (S (p _ {i}) - S (p _ {j})) \right| \prod_ {j ^ {\prime} \in A / \{j \}} S _ {\infty} (S (p _ {i}) - S (p _ {j ^ {\prime}})) \right] S ^ {\prime} (p _ {i}) \\ \leq 6 \sum_ {j \in A} \left| S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime} (S (p _ {i}) - S (p _ {j})) \right| \cdot \prod_ {(j ^ {\prime}, \ell) \neq (j, i)} S _ {\infty} (S (p _ {\ell}) - S (p _ {j ^ {\prime}})) \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality follows by the fact that  $|S'(\cdot)| \leq 6$ . Since  $|A| \leq d$  the proof of the lemma will be completed if we are able to show that for any  $j \in A$ , it holds that

$$
\left| S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime} (S (p _ {i}) - S (p _ {j})) \right| \cdot \prod_ {(j ^ {\prime}, \ell) \neq (j, i)} S _ {\infty} (S (p _ {\ell}) - S (p _ {j ^ {\prime}})) \leq \Theta (d ^ {1 0}) \cdot \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})
$$

In case  $S(p_i) - S(p_j) \geq 1 / d^5$  then by case 3. of Lemma C.5 we get that  $\left| S_{\infty}'(S(p_i) - S(p_j)) \right| \leq c \cdot d^{10} \cdot S_{\infty}(S(p_i) - S(p_j))$ , which implies gthe following

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime} \left(S \left(p _ {i}\right) - S \left(p _ {j}\right)\right) \right| \cdot \prod_ {\left(j ^ {\prime}, \ell\right) \neq \left(j, i\right)} S _ {\infty} \left(S \left(p _ {\ell}\right) - S \left(p _ {j ^ {\prime}}\right)\right) \leq \\ \leq c \cdot d ^ {1 0} \cdot S _ {\infty} (S (p _ {i}) - S (p _ {j})) \cdot \prod_ {(j ^ {\prime}, \ell) \neq (j, i)} S _ {\infty} (S (p _ {\ell}) - S (p _ {j ^ {\prime}})) \\ = c \cdot d ^ {1 0} \cdot Q _ {v} (x) \\ \leq c \cdot d ^ {1 0} \cdot \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x}) \\ \end{array}
$$

Now consider the case where  $S(p_{i}) - S(p_{j}) \leq 1 / d^{5}$ . Using case 2. of Lemma C.5, we have that

$$
\left| S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime} (S (p _ {i}) - S (p _ {j})) \right| \cdot \prod_ {(j ^ {\prime}, \ell) \neq (j, i)} S _ {\infty} (S (p _ {\ell}) - S (p _ {j ^ {\prime}})) \leq \left| S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime} (S (p _ {i}) - S (p _ {j})) \right| \leq \Theta (d ^ {1 0} \cdot 2 ^ {- d ^ {5}})
$$

Consider the sequence of points in the  $[0,1]$  interval  $0, p_1, \ldots, p_d, 1$ . There always exist two consecutive points with distance greater than  $1/(d+1)$ . As a result, there exists  $v^* \in L_c(c)$  such that  $p_\ell - p_j \geq 1/(d+1)$  for all  $\ell \in B_{v^*}$  and  $j \in A_{v^*}$ . Then  $S(p_\ell) - S(p_j) \geq 1/(d+1)^2$  and by case 1. Of Lemma C.5,  $S_\infty(S(p_\ell) - S(p_j)) \geq c2^{-(d+1)^2}$ . If we also use the fact that  $|A_{v^*}| \cdot |B_{v^*}| \leq d^2$ , we get that

$$
Q _ {v ^ {*}} (\boldsymbol {x}) \geq (c \cdot 2 ^ {- (d + 1) ^ {2}}) ^ {d ^ {2}} = c ^ {d ^ {2}} 2 ^ {- (d + 1) ^ {2} \cdot d ^ {2}}.
$$

Then it holds that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{Q _ {v ^ {*}} (\boldsymbol {x})} \cdot \left| S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime} (S (p _ {i}) - S (p _ {j})) \right| \cdot \prod_ {(j ^ {\prime}, \ell) \neq (j, i)} S _ {\infty} (S (p _ {\ell}) - S (p _ {j ^ {\prime}})) \leq \\ \leq \Theta \left(d ^ {1 0} \cdot \left((1 / c) \cdot 2 ^ {- d ^ {3} + (d + 1) ^ {2}}\right) ^ {d ^ {2}}\right) \leq \Theta (d ^ {1 0}). \\ \end{array}
$$

Combining the later with the discussion in the rest of the proof the lemma follows.

Lemma C.7. For any vertex  $\boldsymbol{v} \in ([N] - 1)^d$  it holds that  $\left|\frac{\partial \mathrm{P}_v(\boldsymbol{x})}{\partial x_i}\right| \leq \Theta \left(d^{12} / \delta\right)$ .

Proof. To simplify notation we use  $Q_v(x)$  instead of  $Q_v^c(x)$  for the rest of the proof. Without loss of generality, we assume that  $x$  lies on a cubelet  $L(c)$  with  $c \in ([N] - 1)^d$  and  $v \in L_c(c)$ , since otherwise  $\frac{\partial P_v(x)}{\partial x_i} = 0$ . Let  $p_x^c = (p_1, \ldots, p_d)$  be the canonical representation of  $x$  in the cubelet  $L(c)$ . Then it holds that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {\partial \mathrm {P} _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right| = \frac {\left| \frac {\partial Q _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \cdot \left[ \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] - Q _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x}) \cdot \left[ \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} \frac {\partial Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right] \right|}{(\sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})) ^ {2}} \\ \leq \frac {\left| \frac {\partial Q _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right|}{\sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})} + \frac {\sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} \left| \frac {\partial Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right|}{\sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})} \\ \leq (d + 2) \cdot \Theta (d ^ {1 1}) = \Theta (d ^ {1 2}) \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality follows by Lemma C.6 and the fact that at most  $d + 1$  vertices  $\mathbf{v}$  of  $L_{c}(\pmb{c})$  have non-zero gradient as we have proved in Lemma 8.10. Then the proof of Lemma C.7 follows by the fact that  $p_i = \frac{x_i - s_i}{t_i - s_i}$ .

Lemma C.8. Let  $\pmb{c} \in ([N] - 1)^d$  and  $\pmb{v} \in L_{c}(\pmb{c})$  then it holds that  $\left|\frac{\partial^2 Q_v^c(\pmb{x})}{\partial p_i \partial p_j}\right| \leq \Theta(d^{22}) \cdot \sum_{\pmb{v} \in R_c(\pmb{x})} Q_v^c(\pmb{x})$ .

Proof. To simplify the notation we use  $CS(p_{\ell} - p_{m})$  to denote  $S_{\infty}(S(p_{\ell}) - S(p_{m}))$ ,  $CS'(p_{\ell} - p_{m})$  to denote  $|S_{\infty}'(S(p_{\ell}) - S(p_{m}))|$ ,  $A$  to denote  $A_{v}^{c}$  and  $B$  to denote  $B_{v}^{c}$  for the rest of the proof. As in Lemma C.7, we assume that  $p_{\ell} > p_{m}$  for all  $\ell \in B$  and  $m \in A$  since otherwise  $\frac{\partial^2 Q_v(x)}{\partial p_i \partial p_j} = 0$ . We have the following cases for the indices  $i$  and  $j$

If  $i,j\in B$  then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {\partial^ {2} \mathrm {Q} _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i} \partial p _ {j}} \right| = \\ = \sum_ {m _ {1}, m _ {2} \in A} C S ^ {\prime} (p _ {i} - p _ {m _ {1}}) C S ^ {\prime} (p _ {j} - p _ {m _ {2}}) \cdot \prod_ {(m, \ell) \neq \{(m _ {1}, i), (m _ {2}, j) \}} C S (p _ {\ell} - p _ {m}) \cdot S ^ {\prime} (p _ {i}) S ^ {\prime} (p _ {j}) \\ \leq 36\sum_{m_{1},m_{2}\in A} \underbrace{CS^{\prime}(p_{i} - p_{m_{1}})CS^{\prime}(p_{j} - p_{m_{2}})\cdot\prod_{(m,\ell)\neq\{(m_{1},i),(m_{2},j)\}}CS(p_{\ell}- p_{m})}_{\triangleq U(i,j)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

If additionally it holds that  $S(p_{i}) - S(p_{m_{1}}) \leq 1 / d^{5}$  or  $S(p_{j}) - S(p_{m_{2}}) \leq 1 / d^{5}$ , then by the case 2. of Lemma C.5, we have that

$$
U (i, j) \leq C S ^ {\prime} (p _ {i} - p _ {m _ {1}}) \cdot C S ^ {\prime} (p _ {j} - p _ {m _ {2}}) \leq \Theta (d ^ {1 0} e ^ {- d ^ {5}}).
$$

The latter follows from the fact that the function  $S_{\infty}^{\prime}(\cdot)$  is bounded in the  $[0,1]$  interval and that  $CS(p_{\ell} - p_{m}) \leq 1$ . With the exact same arguments as in Lemma C.6, we hence get that

$$
C S ^ {\prime} \left(p _ {i} - p _ {m _ {1}}\right) C S ^ {\prime} \left(p _ {j} - p _ {m _ {2}}\right) \cdot \Pi_ {(m, \ell) \neq \{(m _ {1}, i), (m _ {2}, j) \}} C S \left(p _ {\ell} - p _ {m}\right) \leq \Theta \left(d ^ {1 0}\right) \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} ^ {\boldsymbol {c}} (\boldsymbol {x}).
$$

Thus  $\left|\frac{\partial^2Q_v(x)}{\partial p_i\partial p_j}\right|\leq \Theta (d^{12})\sum_{\pmb {v}'\in L_c(\pmb {c})}Q_{\pmb{v}''}^c (\pmb {x}).$

On the other hand if  $S(p_i) - S(p_{m_1}) \geq 1 / d^5$  and  $S(p_j) - S(p_{m_2}) \geq 1 / d^5$  then by case 1. of Lemma C.5,  $CS'(p_i - p_{m_1}) \leq c \cdot d^{10} \cdot CS(p_i - p_{m_1})$  and  $CS'(p_j - p_{m_2}) \leq c \cdot d^{10} \cdot CS(p_j - p_{m_2})$  and thus  $U(i,j) \leq \Theta(d^{20}) \cdot Q_v^c(\boldsymbol{x})$ . Overall we get that  $\left| \frac{\partial^2 Q_v(\boldsymbol{x})}{\partial p_i \partial p_j} \right| \leq \Theta(d^{22}) \cdot \sum_{\boldsymbol{v}' \in R_c(\boldsymbol{x})} Q_{v'}^c(\boldsymbol{x})$ .

If  $i\in B$  and  $j\in A$  then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {\partial^ {2} Q _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i} \partial p _ {j}} \right| \leq \\ \leq \sum_ {m _ {1} \in A, \ell_ {2} \in B} C S ^ {\prime} (p _ {i} - p _ {m _ {1}}) C S ^ {\prime} (p _ {\ell_ {2}} - p _ {j}) \cdot \prod_ {(m, \ell) \neq \{(i, m _ {1}), (\ell_ {2}, j) \}} C S (p _ {\ell} - p _ {m}) \cdot S ^ {\prime} (p _ {i}) S ^ {\prime} (p _ {j}) \\ + \left| C S ^ {\prime \prime} \left(p _ {i} - p _ {j}\right) \cdot \prod_ {(m, \ell) \neq (i, j)} C S \left(p _ {\ell} - p _ {m}\right) \cdot S ^ {\prime} \left(p _ {i}\right) S ^ {\prime} \left(p _ {j}\right) \right| \\ \leq \Theta (d ^ {2 2}) \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x}) + 3 6 \underbrace {\left| C S ^ {\prime \prime} (p _ {i} - p _ {j}) \cdot \prod_ {(m , \ell) \neq (i , j)} C S (p _ {\ell} - p _ {m}) \right|} _ {Q ^ {\prime \prime} (\boldsymbol {x})}. \\ \end{array}
$$

In case  $S(p_i) - S(p_j) \geq 1 / d^5$  then by case 4. of Lemma C.5, we get that  $|CS''(p_i - p_j)| \leq c d^{20} \cdot CS(p_i - p_j)$  which implies that  $Q'' \leq \Theta(d^{20}) \cdot Q_v^c(\boldsymbol{x})$ .

On the other hand if  $S(p_{i}) - S(p_{j}) \leq 1 / d^{5}$  then by case 5. of Lemma C.5, we get that  $Q'' \leq \left| CS''(p_{i} - p_{j}) \right| \leq c \cdot d^{20} e^{-d^{5}}$ . As in the proof of Lemma C.6, there exists a vertex  $v^{*} \in R_{c}(x)$  such that  $Q_{v^{*}}^{c}(x) \geq c^{d^{2}} e^{-(d + 1)^{2} d^{2}}$  and thus  $Q'' \leq \Theta(d^{20}) \sum_{v \in L_{c}(c)} Q_{v}^{c}(x)$ . Overall we get that

$$
\left| \frac {\partial^ {2} Q _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i} \partial p _ {j}} \right| \leq \Theta (d ^ {2 2}) \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x}).
$$

If  $i = j\in B$  then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {\partial^ {2} \mathrm {Q} _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial^ {2} p _ {i}} \right| \leq \\ \leq \sum_ {m _ {1}, m _ {2} \in A} \left| C S ^ {\prime} \left(p _ {i} - p _ {m _ {1}}\right) C S ^ {\prime} \left(p _ {i} - p _ {m _ {2}}\right) \cdot \prod_ {(m, \ell) \neq \{(m _ {1}, i), (m _ {2}, i) \}} C S \left(p _ {\ell} - p _ {m}\right) \cdot S ^ {\prime} \left(p _ {i}\right) S ^ {\prime} \left(p _ {i}\right) \right| \\ + \sum_ {m _ {1} \in A} \left| C S ^ {\prime \prime} \left(p _ {i} - p _ {m _ {1}}\right) \cdot \prod_ {(m, \ell) \neq (m _ {1}, \ell)} C S \left(p _ {\ell} - p _ {m}\right) S ^ {\prime} \left(p _ {i}\right) S ^ {\prime} \left(p _ {i}\right) \right| \\ \leq \Theta (d ^ {2 2} + d \cdot d ^ {2 0}) \cdot \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {v} ^ {\boldsymbol {c}} (\boldsymbol {x}). \\ \end{array}
$$

If we combine all the above cases then the Lemma follows.

Lemma C.9. For any vertex  $\pmb{v} \in ([N] - 1)^d$ , it holds that  $\left|\frac{\partial^2 \mathsf{P}_v(x)}{\partial x_i \partial x_j}\right| \leq \Theta(d^{24} / \delta^2)$ .

Proof. Without loss of generality we assume that  $\pmb{v} \in L_{c}(\pmb{c})$ , where  $\pmb{c} \in ([N - 1] - 1)^d$  such that  $x \in L(c)$ , since otherwise  $\frac{\partial^2 \mathsf{P}_v(x)}{\partial x_i \partial x_j} = 0$ .

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\partial^ {2} \mathrm {P} _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i} \partial p _ {j}} = \frac {\partial^ {2} Q _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i} \partial p _ {j}} \left(\sum_ {\boldsymbol {v ^ {\prime}} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})\right) ^ {3} \cdot \frac {1}{\left(\sum_ {\boldsymbol {v ^ {\prime}} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})\right) ^ {4}} \\ + \frac {\partial Q _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v ^ {\prime}} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} \frac {\partial Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {j}} \left(\sum_ {\boldsymbol {v ^ {\prime}} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})\right) ^ {2} \cdot \frac {1}{\left(\sum_ {\boldsymbol {v ^ {\prime}} \in L _ {c} (\boldsymbol {c})} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})\right) ^ {4}} \\ - \frac {\partial Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {j}} \sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} \frac {\partial Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \left(\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})\right) ^ {2} \cdot \frac {1}{\left(\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (\boldsymbol {x})\right) ^ {4}} \\ - Q _ {v} (x) \sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} \frac {\partial^ {2} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (x)}{\partial p _ {i} \partial p _ {j}} \left(\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (x)\right) ^ {2} \cdot \frac {1}{\left(\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (x)\right) ^ {4}} \\ - \frac {\partial Q _ {v} (x)}{\partial p _ {i}} \sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (x) \cdot 2 \sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (x) \sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} \frac {\partial Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (x)}{\partial p _ {j}} \cdot \frac {1}{\left(\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (x)\right) ^ {4}} \\ + Q _ {v} (x) \sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} \frac {\partial Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (x)}{\partial p _ {i}} \cdot 2 \sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (x) \sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} \frac {\partial Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (x)}{\partial p _ {j}} \cdot \frac {1}{\left(\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in L _ {c} (c)} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} (x)\right) ^ {4}} \\ \end{array}
$$

Using Lemma C.8 and Lemma C.6 we can bound every term in the above expression and hence we get that  $\left|\frac{\partial^2\mathrm{P}_v(x)}{\partial p_i\partial p_j}\right| \leq \Theta (d^{24})$ . Then the lemma follows from the fact that  $\frac{\partial p_i}{\partial x_i} = 1 / \delta$ .

Finally using Lemma C.7 and Lemma C.9 we get the proof of Lemma 8.11.

# C.3 Proof of Lemma 8.12

Let  $0 \leq x_{i} < 1 / (N - 1)$  and  $c = (c_{1},\ldots ,c_{i},\ldots ,c_{d})$  denote down-left corner of the cubelet  $R(x)$  at which  $x \in [0,1]^d$  lies, i.e.  $x \in L(c)$ . Since  $x \leq 1 / (N - 1)$ , this means that  $c_{i} = 0$ . By the definition of sources and targets in Definition 8.6, we have that  $s_i = 0$  and  $t_i = 1 / (N - 1)$ , where  $s_i, t_i$  are respectively the  $i$ -th coordinate of the source  $s_c$  and the target  $t_c$  vertex. Let the canonical representation  $p_x^c = (p_1,\dots ,p_d)$  of  $x$  in the cubelet  $L(c)$ . Now partition the coordinates  $[d]$  in the following sets

$$
A = \left\{j \mid p _ {j} \leq p _ {i} \right\} \quad \text {a n d} \quad B = \left\{j \mid p _ {i} <   p _ {j} \right\}.
$$

If  $B = \varnothing$  then notice that  $\mathsf{P}_{s_c}(x) > 0$ , since  $p_i < 1$ , by the fact that  $x_i < 1 / (N - 1)$ . Thus the lemma follows since  $s_i = 0$ . So we may assume that  $B \neq \varnothing$ . In this case consider the corner  $v = (v_1, \ldots, v_d)$  defined as follows

$$
v _ {j} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} s _ {j} & j \in A \\ t _ {j} & j \in B \end{array} \right..
$$

Observe that  $Q_{v}^{c}(x) > 0$  and thus  $v \in R_{+}(x)$ . Moreover the coordinate  $i \in A$  and therefore it holds that  $v_{i} = s_{i} = 0$ . This proves the first statement of the Lemma.

For the second statement let  $1 - 1 / (N - 1) \leq x_{i} \leq 1 / (N - 1)$  and  $c = (c_{1},\ldots ,c_{i},\ldots ,c_{d})$  denote down-left corner of the cubelet  $R(x)$  at which  $x \in [0,1]^d$  lies, i.e.  $x \in L(c)$ . This means that  $c_{i} = \frac{N - 2}{N - 1}$ .

Let  $N$  be odd. In this case by the definition of sources and targets in Definition 8.6, we have that  $s_i = 1 - 1 / (N - 1)$  and  $t_i = 1$ , where  $s_i, t_i$  are respectively the  $i$ -th coordinate of the source and target vertex. Let  $p_x^c = (p_1, \ldots, p_d)$  be the canonical representation of  $x$  under in the cubelet  $L(c)$ . Now partition the coordinates  $[d]$  as follows,

$$
A = \left\{j \mid p _ {j} <   p _ {i} \right\} \quad \text {a n d} \quad B = \left\{j \mid p _ {i} \leq p _ {j} \right\}
$$

If  $A = \varnothing$  then notice that for the target vertex  $t_c$ ,  $\mathsf{P}_{t_c}(x) > 0$ , since  $p_i > 0$ , by the fact that  $x_i > 1 - 1 / (N - 1)$ . Thus the lemma follows since  $t_i = 1$ . So we may assume that  $A \neq \emptyset$ . In this case consider the corner  $v = (v_1, \ldots, v_d)$  defined as follows,

$$
v _ {j} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} s _ {j} & j \in A \\ t _ {j} & j \in B \end{array} \right.
$$

Observe that  $Q_{v}^{c}(x) > 0$  and thus  $v \in R_{+}(x)$ . Moreover the coordinate  $i \in B$  and thus  $v_{i} = t_{i} = 1$ .

Let  $N$  be even. In this case we have that  $t_i = 1 - 1 / (N - 1)$  and  $s_i = 1$ . Now partition the coordinates  $[d]$  as follows,

$$
A = \left\{j \mid p _ {j} \leq p _ {i} \right\} \quad \text {a n d} \quad B = \left\{j \mid p _ {i} <   p _ {j} \right\}
$$

If  $B = \varnothing$  then notice that for the source vertex  $s_c$ ,  $\mathsf{P}_{s_c}(x) > 0$ , since  $p_i < 1$ , by the fact that  $x_i > 1 - 1 / (N - 1)$ . Thus the lemma follows since  $s_i = 1$ . In case  $B \neq \varnothing$  consider the corner  $v = (v_1, \ldots, v_d)$  defined as follows,

$$
v _ {j} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} s _ {j} & j \in A \\ t _ {j} & j \in B \end{array} \right.
$$

Observe that  $Q_{v}^{c}(\pmb{x}) > 0$  and thus  $\pmb{v} \in R_{+}(\pmb{x})$ . Moreover the coordinate  $i \in A$  and thus  $v_{i} = s_{i} = 1$ .

If we put together the last two cases then this implies the second statement of the lemma.

# D Constructing the Turing Machine - Proof of Theorem 7.6

In this section we prove Theorem 7.6 establishing that both the function  $f_{\mathcal{C}_l}(x,y)$  of Definition 7.4 and its gradient, is computable by a polynomial-time Turing Machine. We prove Theorem 7.6 through a series of Lemmas. To simplify notation we set  $b \triangleq \log 1 / \varepsilon$ .

Definition D.1. For a  $x \in \mathbb{R}$ , we denote by  $[x]_b \in \mathbb{R}$ , a value represented by the  $b$  bits such that

$$
\left| \left[ x \right] _ {b} - x \right| \leq 2 ^ {- b}.
$$

Lemma D.2. There exist Turing Machines  $M_{S_{\infty}}$ ,  $M_{S_{\infty}'}$  that given input  $x \in [0,1]$  and  $\varepsilon$  in binary form, compute  $[S_{\infty}(x)]_b$  and  $[S_{\infty}'(x)]_b$  in time polynomial in  $b = \log (1 / \varepsilon)$  and the binary representation of  $x$ .

Proof. The Turing Machine  $M_{S_{\infty}}$  outputs the fist  $b$  bits of the following quantity,

$$
W (x) = \left[ \frac {1}{1 + \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ - \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}
$$

where  $b'$  will be selected sufficiently large. Notice it is possible to compute the above quantity due to the fact that all functions  $\frac{1}{\gamma} + \frac{1}{\gamma - 1}$ ,  $2^{\gamma}$  and  $\frac{1}{1 + \gamma}$  can be computed with accuracy  $2^{-b'}$  in polynomial time with respect to  $b'$  and the binary representation of  $\gamma$  [Bre76]. Moreover,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left[ \frac {1}{1 + \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ - \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \frac {1}{1 + 2 ^ {- \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1}}} \right| \\ \leq \left| \left[ \frac {1}{1 + \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ - \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \frac {1}{1 + \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ - \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right| \\ + \left| \frac {1}{1 + \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ - \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} - \frac {1}{1 + 2 ^ {\left[ - \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}}} \right| \\ + \left| \frac {1}{1 + 2 ^ {\left[ - \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}}} - \frac {1}{1 + 2 ^ {- \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1}}} \right| \\ \leq 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}} + \left| \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ - \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - 2 ^ {\left[ - \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right| \\ + \ln 2 \left| \left[ - \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \left(- \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1}\right) \right| \\ \leq 4 \cdot 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality follows from triangle inequality and the second follows from the facts that  $1 / (1 + \gamma)$  is a 1-Lipschitz function of  $\gamma$  for  $\gamma \geq 0$ , and  $1 / (1 + 2^{\gamma})$  is an  $\ln(2)$ -Lipschitz function of  $\gamma$  for  $\gamma \geq 0$ . The last inequality follows from the definition of  $[\cdot]_{b'}$ . Hence  $W(x)$  is indeed equal to  $[S_{\infty}(x)]_b$  if we choose  $b' = b + 2$ .

Next we explain how  $M_{S_{\infty}^{\prime}}$  computes  $[S_{\infty}^{\prime}(x)]_b$ . First notice that  $S_{\infty}^{\prime}(x)$  is equal to

$$
S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime} (x) = \ln 2 \cdot \frac {\frac {1}{x ^ {2}} 2 ^ {- \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1}} - \frac {1}{(x - 1) ^ {2}} 2 ^ {- \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1}}}{\left(2 ^ {- \frac {1}{x}} + 2 ^ {\frac {1}{x - 1}}\right) ^ {2}}.
$$

To describe how to compute  $S_{\infty}'(x)$  we first assume that we have computed the following quantities. Then based on these quantities we show how  $S_{\infty}'(x)$  can be computed and finally we consider the computation of these quantities.

$$
\begin{array}{l} \triangleright [ \ln 2 ] _ {b ^ {\prime}}, \\ \triangleright A \leftarrow \left[ \frac {1}{x ^ {2}} 2 ^ {- \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}, \\ \triangleright B \gets \left[ \frac {1}{(x - 1) ^ {2}} 2 ^ {- \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}, \\ \triangleright C \leftarrow \left[ \left(2 ^ {- \frac {1}{x}} + 2 ^ {\frac {1}{x - 1}}\right) ^ {2} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

Then  $M_{S_{\infty}^{\prime}}$  outputs the fist  $b$  bits of the quantity  $\left[\left[\ln 2\right]_{b^{\prime}} \cdot \left[\frac{A + B}{C}\right]_{b^{\prime}}\right]_{b^{\prime}}$ . We now prove that

$$
\left| \left[ \ln 2 \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \left[ \frac {A + B}{C} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \underbrace {\ln 2 \frac {A + B}{C}} _ {S _ {\infty} ^ {\prime} (x)} \right| \leq \Theta \left(2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}}\right)
$$

Consider the function  $g(\alpha, \beta, \gamma) = \frac{\alpha + \beta}{\gamma}$  where  $|\alpha|, |\beta| \leq c_1$  and  $|\gamma| \geq c_2$  where  $c_1, c_2$  are universal constants. Notice that  $g(\alpha, \beta, \gamma)$  is  $c$ -Lipschitz for  $c = \sqrt{\frac{2}{c_2^2} + \frac{2c_1}{c_2^2}}$ . Since for sufficiently large  $b'$  all the quantities  $|A|, |B|, \left|\frac{1}{x^2} 2^{-\frac{1}{x} + \frac{1}{x - 1}}\right|, \left|\frac{1}{(x - 1)^2} 2^{-\frac{1}{x} + \frac{1}{x - 1}}\right| \leq c_1$  and  $|C|, \left(2^{-\frac{1}{x}} + 2^{\frac{1}{x - 1}}\right)^2 \geq c_2$  where  $c_1, c_2$  are universal constants we get that

$$
\left| \left[ \frac {A + B}{C} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \frac {A + B}{C} \right| \leq \Theta \left(2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}}\right).
$$

Now consider the function  $g(\alpha, \beta) = \alpha \cdot \beta$  where  $|\alpha|, |\beta| \leq c$  where  $c$  is a universal constant. In this case  $g(\alpha, \beta)$  is  $\sqrt{2}c$ -Lipschitz continuous. Since for  $b'$  sufficiently large all the quantities  $\left|\left[\ln 2\right]_{b'}\right|, \left|\left[\frac{A + B}{C}\right]_{b'}\right|, \ln 2, \left|\frac{A + B}{C}\right|$  are bounded by a universal constant  $c$ , we have that,

$$
\left| \left[ \ln 2 \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \left[ \frac {A + B}{C} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \ln 2 \frac {A + B}{C} \right| \leq \Theta \left(2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}}\right)
$$

Next we explain how the values  $A, B$  and  $C$  are computed while  $[\ln(2)]_b'$  can easily be computed via standard techniques [Bre76].

$\triangleright$  Computation of  $A$ . The Turing Machine  $M_{S_{\infty}^{\prime}}$  will compute  $A$  by taking the first  $b^{\prime}$  bits of the following quantity,

$$
\left. \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ - \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1} + 2 \ln x / \ln 2 \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} \right.
$$

where  $b''$  will be taken sufficiently large. We remark that both where both the exponentiation and the natural logarithm can be computed in polynomial-time with respect to the number of accuracy bits and the binary representation of the input [Bre76]. The function  $\frac{1}{x^2} 2^{-\frac{1}{x} + \frac{1}{x - 1}} = 2^{-\frac{1}{x} + \frac{1}{x - 1} + 2\ln x / \ln 2}$  is  $c$ -Lipschitz where  $c$  is a universal constant. Thus,

$$
\left| \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ - \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1} + 2 \ln x / \ln 2 \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} - \frac {1}{x ^ {2}} 2 ^ {- \frac {1}{x} + \frac {1}{x - 1}} \right| \leq \Theta (2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime \prime}}).
$$

$\triangleright$  Computation of  $B$ . Using the same arguments as for  $A$ .  
$\triangleright$  Computation of  $C$ . To compute  $C$  we first compute  $b''$  bits of the following quantity,

$$
\left[ \frac {1}{\left[ 2 ^ {- \left[ \frac {1}{x} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} + \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} ^ {2}
$$

We first argue that

$$
\left| \left[ \frac {1}{\left[ 2 ^ {- \left[ \frac {1}{x} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} + \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} ^ {2} - \left(\frac {1}{2 ^ {- \frac {1}{x}} + 2 ^ {\frac {1}{x - 1}}}\right) ^ {2} \right| \leq \Theta (2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime \prime}})
$$

The latter follows by applying the triangle inequality and the following 3 inequalities.

1.

$$
\left| \left[ \frac {1}{\left[ 2 ^ {- \left[ \frac {1}{x} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} + \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} ^ {2} - \left(\frac {1}{\left[ 2 ^ {- \left[ \frac {1}{x} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} + \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}}\right) ^ {2} \right| \leq \Theta (2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime \prime}})
$$

this holds since for  $b'' > 1$  we have

$$
\left[ \frac {1}{\left(\left[ 2 ^ {- \left[ \frac {1}{x} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} + \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}\right)} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} \quad \text {a n d} \quad \frac {1}{\left(\left[ 2 ^ {- \left[ \frac {1}{x} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} + \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}\right)}
$$

are both upper-bounded by 2 while the function  $g(\alpha) = \alpha^2$  is 4-Lipschitz for  $|\alpha| \leq 2$ .

2.

$$
\left| \left(\frac {1}{\left[ 2 ^ {- \left[ \frac {1}{x} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}} + \left[ 2 ^ {\left[ \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}}\right) ^ {2} - \left(\frac {1}{2 ^ {- \left[ \frac {1}{x} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} + 2 ^ {\left[ \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}}}\right) ^ {2} \right| \leq \Theta (2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime \prime}})
$$

The latter follows since for  $b''$  larger than a universal constant, both  $\left[2^{-\left[\frac{1}{x}\right]_{b''}}\right]_{b''} + \left[2^{\left[\frac{1}{x - 1}\right]_{b''}}\right]_{b''}$  and  $2^{-\left[\frac{1}{x}\right]_{b''}} + 2^{\left[\frac{1}{x - 1}\right]_{b''}}$  are greater than a universal constant  $c$ , while the function  $g(\alpha, \beta) = 1 / (\alpha + \beta)^2$  is  $\Theta(c^3)$ -Lipschitz for  $\alpha + \beta \geq c$ .

3.

$$
\left| \left(\frac {1}{2 ^ {- \left[ \frac {1}{x} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}} + 2 ^ {\left[ \frac {1}{x - 1} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime \prime}}}}\right) ^ {2} - \left(\frac {1}{2 ^ {- \frac {1}{x}} + 2 ^ {\frac {1}{x - 1}}}\right) ^ {2} \right| \leq \Theta (2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime \prime}})
$$

The latter follows since for  $b''$  larger than a universal constant it holds that both the quantities in the left hand side are greater than a positive universal constant  $c$ , while the function  $g(\alpha, \beta) = 1 / (2^{-\alpha} + 2^{\beta})$  for  $2^{-\alpha} + 2^{\beta} \geq c$ ,  $\alpha \geq 0$ , and  $\beta \leq 0$  is  $\Theta(1/c^3)$ -Lipschitz.

This concludes the proof of the lemma.

Lemma D.3. There exist Turing Machines  $M_Q$  and  $M_{Q'}$  that given  $\pmb{x} \in [0,1]^d$  and  $\varepsilon > 0$  in binary form, respectively compute  $[Q_v^c(\pmb{x})]_b$  and  $[\nabla Q_v^c(\pmb{x})]_b$  for all vertices  $\pmb{v} \in ([N] - 1)^d$  with  $Q_v^c(\pmb{x}) > 0$ , where  $b = \log(1 / \varepsilon)$ . These vertices are most  $d + 1$ . Moreover both  $M_Q$  and  $M_{Q'}$  run in polynomial time with respect to  $b$ ,  $d$  and the binary representation of  $\pmb{x}$ .

Proof. Both  $M_Q, M_{Q'}$  firsts compute the canonical representation  $p_x^c \in [0,1]^d$  with the respect to the cell  $R(x)$  in which  $x$  lies. Such a cell  $R(x)$  can be computed by taking the first  $(\log N + 1)$ -bits at each coordinate of  $x$ . The source vertex  $s^c = (s_1, \ldots, s_d)$  and the target vertex  $t^c = (t_1, \ldots, t_d)$  with respect to  $R(x)$  are also computed. Once this is done we are only interested in vertices  $v \in R_c(x)$  for which

$$
p _ {\ell} > p _ {j} \quad \text {f o r a l l} \ell \in A _ {v ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} j \in B _ {v} ^ {c}
$$

since for all the other  $\pmb{v} \in ([N] - 1)^d$  both  $Q_v^c(\pmb{x}) = 0$  and  $\nabla Q_v^c(\pmb{x}) = 0$ . These vertices, that are denoted by  $R_+(x)$ , are at most  $d + 1$  and can be computed in polynomial time.

The vertices  $\pmb{v} \in R_{+}(\pmb{x})$  can be computed in polynomial time as follows: (i) the coordinates  $p_1, \ldots, p_d$  are sorted in increasing order ii) for each  $m = 0, \ldots, d$  compute the vertex  $\pmb{v}^{m} \in R_{c}(\pmb{x})$ ,

$$
\boldsymbol {v} _ {j} ^ {m} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} s _ {j} & \text {i f c o o r d i n a t e j b e l o n g s i n t h e f i r s t m c o o r d i n a t e s w r t t h e o r d e r o f p _ {x} ^ {c}} \\ t _ {j} & \text {i f c o o r d i n a t e j b e l o n g s i n t h e l a s t d - m c o o r d i n a t e s w r t t h e o r d e r o f p _ {x} ^ {c}} \end{array} \right.
$$

By Definition 8.7 it immediately follows that  $R_{+}(\pmb{x}) \subseteq \bigcup_{m=0}^{d} \{v^{m}\}$  which also establish that  $|R_{+}(\pmb{x})| \leq d + 1$ .

Once  $R_{+}(\pmb{x})$  is computed,  $M_{Q}$  computes for each pair  $(\ell, j) \in B_{\pmb{v}}^{c} \times A_{\pmb{v}}^{c}$  the value of the number  $\left[S_{\infty}(S(p_{\ell}) - S(p_{j}))\right]_{b'}$  for some accuracy  $b'$  that we determine later but depends polynomially on  $b, d$  and the input accuracy of  $x$ . Then each  $v \in R_{+}(\pmb{x})$ ,  $M_{Q}$  outputs as  $[Q_{v}^{c}(\pmb{x})]_{b}$  the fist  $b$  bits of the following quantity

$$
\left[ \prod_ {\ell \in B _ {v} ^ {c}, j \in A _ {v} ^ {c}} \left[ S _ {\infty} (S (p _ {\ell}) - S (p _ {j})) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}
$$

where  $b'$  is selected sufficiently large. We next prove that this computation indeed outputs  $[Q_v^c(x)]_b$  accurately.

To simplify notation let  $S_{\infty}(S(p_{\ell}) - S(p_{j}))$  be denoted by  $S_{\ell j}$ ,  $A_v^c$  denoted by  $A$  and  $B_v^c$  denoted by  $B$ . Then,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left[ \Pi_ {\ell \in B, j \in A} \left[ S _ {\ell j} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \Pi_ {\ell \in B, j \in A} S _ {\ell j} \right| \leq \left| \left[ \Pi_ {\ell \in B, j \in A} \left[ S _ {\ell j} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \Pi_ {\ell \in B, j \in A} \left[ S _ {\ell j} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \right| \\ { + } { \left| \Pi _ { \ell \in B , j \in A } \left[ S _ { \ell j } \right] _ { b ^ { \prime } } - \Pi _ { \ell \in B , j \in A } S _ { \ell j } \right| } \\ \leq 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}} + \left| \Pi_ {\ell \in B, j \in A} \left[ S _ {\ell j} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \Pi_ {\ell \in B, j \in A} S _ {\ell j} \right| \\ \end{array}
$$

Consider the function  $g(\pmb{y}) = \prod_{\ell \in B, j \in A} y_{\ell j}$ . For  $\pmb{y} \in [0, 1 + 1 / d^2]^{|A| \times |B|}$ ,  $\| \nabla g(\pmb{y}) \|_2 \leq \Theta(d)$ . As a result, for all  $\pmb{y}, \pmb{z} \in [0, 1 + 1 / d^2]^{|A| \times |B|}$ ,

$$
| g (\boldsymbol {y}) - g (\boldsymbol {z}) | \leq \Theta (d) \cdot \left[ \sum_ {\ell \in B, j \in A} \left(y _ {\ell j} - z _ {\ell j}\right) \right] ^ {1 / 2}
$$

In case the accuracy  $b' \geq \Theta(\log d)$  then  $\left[ S_{\ell j} \right]_{b'} \leq S_{\ell j} + 1 / d^2 \leq 1 + 1 / d^2$  and the above inequality applies. Thus,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \prod_ {\ell \in B, j \in A} \left[ S _ {\ell j} \right] _ {B ^ {\prime}} - \Pi_ {\ell \in B, j \in A} S _ {\ell j} \right| \leq \Theta (d) \left[ \sum_ {\ell \in B, j \in A} \left(\left[ S _ {\ell j} \right] _ {B ^ {\prime}} - S _ {\ell j}\right) \right] ^ {1 / 2} \\ \leq \Theta (d ^ {2}) \cdot 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}} \\ \end{array}
$$

Overall,  $\left|\left[\Pi_{\ell \in B,j\in A}\left[S_{\ell j}\right]_{b'}\right]_{b'} - \Pi_{\ell \in B,j\in A}S_{\ell j}\right| \leq \Theta (d^2) \cdot 2^{-b'}$  which concludes the proof of the corrected of  $[Q_v^c (\pmb {x})]_b$  by selecting  $b^{\prime} = b + \Theta (\log d)$ .

In order to compute  $\frac{\partial Q_v^c(x)}{\partial x_\ell}$  where  $\ell \in B_v^c$  (symmetrically for  $j \in A_v^c$ ),  $M_{Q'}$  additionally computes the  $[S_{\infty}'(S(p_\ell) - S(p_j))]_{b'}$  with accuracy  $b'$ . To simplify notation we denote with  $S_{\infty}'(S(p_\ell) - S(p_j))$  with  $S_{\ell j}'$  and  $S'(p_i)$  by  $S_i'$ . Then  $M_{Q'}$  outputs,

$$
\left[ \frac {\partial Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial x _ {i}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \gets \left[ \frac {1}{t _ {i} - s _ {i}} \cdot \left[ \frac {\partial Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}
$$

$$
\text {w h e r e} \quad \left[ \frac {\partial Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \leftarrow \left[ \sum_ {j \in A} \left[ S _ {i j} ^ {\prime} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \cdot \left[ S _ {i} ^ {\prime} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \Pi_ {m \in A / j, \ell \in B} \left[ S _ {\ell m} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}
$$

Observe that  $t_i - s_i = \frac{\mathrm{sign}(t_i - s_i)}{N - 1}$  and thus  $\frac{1}{t_i - s_i} \cdot \left[\frac{\partial Q_v^c(x)}{\partial p_i}\right]_{b'}$  can be exactly computed. We next prove that these computations of  $\left[\frac{\partial Q_v^c(x)}{\partial x_i}\right]_{b'}$  and  $\left[\frac{\partial Q_v^c(x)}{\partial p_i}\right]_{b'}$  are correct.

$$
\text {W e f i r s t b o u n d} \left| \left[ S _ {i j} ^ {\prime} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \cdot \left[ S _ {i} ^ {\prime} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \cdot \Pi_ {m \in A / \{j \}, \ell \in B} \left[ S _ {\ell m} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - S _ {i j} ^ {\prime} \cdot S _ {i} ^ {\prime} \cdot \Pi_ {m \in A / \{j \}, \ell \in B} S _ {\ell m} \right|.
$$

Consider the function  $g(y_{1},y_{2},\pmb {y}) = y_{1}\cdot y_{2}\cdot \prod_{m\in A / \{j\} ,\ell \in B}y_{\ell m}$ . As previously done, for  $y_{1},y_{2}\in [0,6]$  and  $\pmb {y}\in [0,1 + 1 / d^{2}]^{|A|\times |B| - 1}$  we have that,  $\| \nabla g(y_1,y_2,\pmb {y})\| _2\leq \Theta (d)$ . If  $b^{\prime}\leq \Theta (\log d)$  then  $\left|S_{ij}^{\prime}\right|,S_i^\prime \leq 6$  and  $S_{\ell m}\in [0,1 + 1 / d^2 ]$ . As a result,

$$
\left| \left[ S _ {i j} ^ {\prime} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \cdot \left[ S _ {i} ^ {\prime} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \cdot \Pi_ {m \in A / \{j \}, \ell \in B} \left[ S _ {\ell m} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - S _ {i j} ^ {\prime} \cdot S _ {i} ^ {\prime} \cdot \Pi_ {m \in A / \{j \}, \ell \in B} S _ {\ell m} \right| \leq \Theta (d ^ {2}) \cdot 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}}.
$$

We can now use the above inequality to bound  $\left|\left[\frac{\partial Q_v^c(x)}{\partial p_i}\right]_{b'} - \frac{\partial Q_v^c(x)}{\partial p_i}\right|$ . More precisely,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left[ \frac {\partial Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \frac {\partial Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right| \\ \leq 2 ^ {- b} + \left| \sum_ {j \in A} \left[ S _ {i j} ^ {\prime} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \cdot \left[ S _ {i} ^ {\prime} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \cdot \prod_ {m \in A / \{j \}, \ell \in B} \left[ S _ {\ell m} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \sum_ {j \in A} S _ {i j} ^ {\prime} \cdot S _ {i} ^ {\prime} \cdot \prod_ {m \in A / \{j \}, \ell \in B} S _ {\ell m} \right| \\ \leq \Theta (d ^ {3}) \cdot 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}} \\ \end{array}
$$

We finally get that

$$
\left| \left[ \frac {\partial Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial x _ {i}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \frac {\partial Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial x _ {i}} \right| \leq 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}} + N \left| \left[ \frac {\partial Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \frac {\partial Q _ {v} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right| \leq \Theta (N d ^ {3}) \cdot 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}}.
$$

Thus the analysis is completed by selecting  $b' = b + \Theta(\log d) + \Theta(\log N)$ .

Lemma D.4. There exist Turing Machines  $M_P$  and  $M_{P'}$  that given  $x \in [0,1]^d$  and  $\varepsilon > 0$  in binary form compute  $[\mathrm{P}_v(x)]_b$  and  $[\nabla \mathrm{P}_v(x)]_b$  respectively for all vertices  $v \in ([N] - 1)^d$  with  $\mathrm{P}_v(x) > 0$ , where  $b = \log(1 / \varepsilon)$ . These vertices are most  $d + 1$ . Moreover both  $M_P$  and  $M_{P'}$  run in polynomial time with respect to  $b$ ,  $d$  and the binary representation of  $x$ .

Proof.  $M_P$  first runs  $M_Q$  of Lemma D.3 to find the coefficients  $Q_v^c(x) > 0$ . We remind that these vertices are denoted with  $R_+(x)$  and  $|R_+(x)| \leq d + 1$ . Then for each  $v \in R_+(x)$ ,  $M_P$  outputs as  $[\mathrm{P}_v(x)]_b$  the fist  $b$  bits of the quantity,

$$
\left[ \frac {\left[ Q _ {v} ^ {c} (x) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}}{\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (x)} \left[ Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (x) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}
$$

where we determine the value of  $b'$  later in the proof but it is chosen to be polynomial in  $b$  and  $d$ . We next present the proof that the above expression correctly computes  $[\mathrm{P}_v(x)]_b$ .

For accuracy  $b' \geq \Theta(d^2 \log d)$  we get that,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (\boldsymbol {x})} \left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \geq \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (\boldsymbol {x})} Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x}) - \Theta (d) \cdot 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}} \\ = \sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in R _ {c} (x)} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (x) - \Theta (d) \cdot 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}} \\ \geq \Theta (1 / d) ^ {d ^ {2}}) - \Theta (d) \cdot 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}} \\ \geq \Theta \left(\left(1 / d\right) ^ {d ^ {2}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

Consider the function  $g(\pmb{y}) = y_i / (\sum_{j=1}^{d+1} y_j)$ . Notice that for  $\pmb{y} \in [0,1]^{d+1}$  and  $\sum_{j=1}^{d+1} y_j \geq \mu$  then  $\| \nabla g(\pmb{y}) \|_2 \leq \Theta(d^{3/2} / \mu^2)$ . The latter implies that for  $\pmb{y}, \pmb{z} \in [0,1]^{d+1}$  such that  $\sum_{j=1}^{d+1} y_j \geq \mu$  and that  $\sum_{j=1}^{d+1} z_j \geq \mu$ , it holds that

$$
\left| \frac {y _ {i}}{\sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d + 1} y _ {j}} - \frac {z _ {i}}{\sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d + 1} z _ {j}} \right| \leq \Theta \left(\frac {d ^ {3 / 2}}{\mu^ {2}}\right) \cdot \| \mathbf {y} - \mathbf {z} \| _ {2}.
$$

Since there are at most  $d + 1$  vertices  $\pmb{v}^{\prime}\in R_{+}(\pmb {x})$  while both the term  $\sum v^{\prime}\in R_{+}(x)\left[Q_{v^{\prime}}^{c}(\pmb {x})\right]_{b^{\prime}}$  and the term  $\sum v^{\prime}\in R_{+}(x)Q_{v^{\prime}}^{c}(x)$  are greater than  $\Theta \left((1 / d)^{d^2}\right)$ , we can apply the above inequality with  $\mu = \Theta \left((1 / d)^{d^2}\right)$  and we get the following

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {\left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {c} (x) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}}{\sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (\boldsymbol {x})} \left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (x) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} - \frac {Q _ {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {c} (x)}{\sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (\boldsymbol {x})} Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (x)} \right| \\ \leq \Theta \left(d ^ {2 d ^ {2} + 3 / 2}\right) \cdot \left[ \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (\boldsymbol {x})} \left(\left[ Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - Q _ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (\boldsymbol {x})\right) ^ {2} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \\ \leq \Theta (d ^ {2 d ^ {2} + 2}) \cdot 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}} \\ \end{array}
$$

Overall, we have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left[ \frac {\left[ Q _ {v} ^ {c} (x) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}}{\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (x)} \left[ Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (x) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \frac {Q _ {v} ^ {c} (x)}{\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in R _ {c} (x)} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (x)} \right| \\ \leq \left| \left[ \frac {\left[ Q _ {v} ^ {c} (x) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}}{\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (x)} \left[ Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (x) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \frac {\left[ Q _ {v} ^ {c} (x) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}}{\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (x)} \left[ Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (x) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} \right| \\ + \left| \frac {\left[ Q _ {v} ^ {c} (x) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}}{\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (x)} \left[ Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (x) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}} - \frac {Q _ {v} ^ {c} (x)}{\sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (x)} Q _ {v ^ {\prime}} ^ {c} (x)} \right| \\ \leq \Theta \left(d ^ {2 d ^ {2} + 1}\right) 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}} \\ \end{array}
$$

The proof is completed via selecting  $b' = b + \Theta(d^2 \log d)$ .

In order to compute  $\frac{\partial P_v(x)}{\partial x_i}$  the Turing machine  $M_{P'}$  computes all vertices  $R_+(x)$  the coefficients  $\frac{\partial Q_v^c(x)}{\partial x_i}$  with accuracy  $b'$ . Then for each  $v \in R_+(x)$  the Turing Machine  $M_{P'}$  outputs,

$$
\left[ \frac {\partial \mathrm {P} _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial x _ {i}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \leftarrow \left[ \frac {1}{t _ {i} - s _ {i}} \cdot \left[ \frac {\partial \mathrm {P} _ {v} (\boldsymbol {x})}{\partial p _ {i}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}}
$$

where  $\left[\frac{\partial\mathrm{P}_v(\boldsymbol{x})}{\partial p_i}\right]_{b'}\gets \left[\frac{\left[\frac{\partial Q_v(\boldsymbol{x})}{\partial p_i}\right]_{b'}\cdot\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}'\in R_+(\boldsymbol{x})}[Q_{\boldsymbol{v}'}(\boldsymbol{x})]_{b'} - [Q_\boldsymbol{v}(\boldsymbol{x})]_{b'}\cdot\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}'\in R_+(\boldsymbol{x})}\left[\frac{\partial Q_{\boldsymbol{v}''}(\boldsymbol{x})}{\partial p_i}\right]_{b'}}{\left(\sum_{\boldsymbol{v}'\in R_+(\boldsymbol{x})}[Q_{\boldsymbol{v}''}(\boldsymbol{x})]_{b'}\right)^2}\right]_{b'}$

Similarly as above and as in Lemma D.3 we can prove that if  $b' \geq b + \Theta(d^2 \log d) + \Theta(\log N)$ ,  $\left| \left[ \frac{\partial P_v(x)}{\partial p_i} \right]_{b'} - \frac{\partial P_v(x)}{\partial p_i} \right| \leq 2^{-b}$ .

Proof of Theorem 7.6. Let  $R(x)$  be the cell at which  $x$  lies. The Turing Machine  $M_{f_{\mathcal{C}_l}}$  initially calculates the vertices  $v \in R_c(x)$  with coefficient  $\mathrm{P}_v(x) > 0$ . We remind that this set is denoted by  $R_+(x)$  and  $|R_+(x)| \leq d + 1$ . Then  $M_{f_{\mathcal{C}_l}}$  outputs the first  $b$  bits of the following quantity,

$$
\left[ f _ {\mathcal {C} _ {l} (x, y)} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d} [ \alpha (x, j) ] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \cdot (x _ {j} - y _ {j}) \quad \text {w h e r e} \quad [ \alpha (x, j) ] _ {b ^ {\prime}} = \sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (x)} \mathcal {C} _ {l} (v, j) \cdot [ P _ {v} (x) ] _ {b ^ {\prime}}
$$

we next prove that the above computation is correct.

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left[ f _ {\mathcal {C} _ {l} (\boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {y})} \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - f _ {\mathcal {C} _ {l} (\boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {y})} \right| = \left| \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d} [ \alpha (\boldsymbol {x}, j) ] _ {b ^ {\prime}} \cdot (x _ {j} - y _ {j}) - \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d} \alpha (\boldsymbol {x}, j) \cdot (x _ {j} - y _ {j}) \right| \\ \leq \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d} | [ \alpha (\boldsymbol {x}, j) ] - \alpha (\boldsymbol {x}, j) | \\ = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d} \left| \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (\boldsymbol {x})} \mathcal {C} _ {l} (\boldsymbol {v}, j) \cdot \left[ \mathrm {P} _ {\boldsymbol {v}} (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \sum_ {\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (\boldsymbol {x})} \mathcal {C} _ {l} (\boldsymbol {v}, j) \cdot \mathrm {P} _ {\boldsymbol {v}} (\boldsymbol {x}) \right| \\ \leq \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {d} \sum_ {v ^ {\prime} \in R _ {+} (x)} | [ \mathrm {P} _ {v} (x) ] _ {b ^ {\prime}} - \mathrm {P} _ {v} (x) | \\ \leq d \cdot (d + 1) \cdot 2 ^ {- b ^ {\prime}} \\ \end{array}
$$

Setting  $b' = b + \Theta (\log d)$  we get the desired result. Similarly for  $\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{C}_l(x,y)}}{\partial x_i}$  and  $\frac{\partial f_{\mathcal{C}_l(x,y)}}{\partial y_i}$ .

# E Convergence of PGD to Approximate Local Minimum

In this section we present for completeness the folklore result that the Projected Gradient Descent with convex projection set converges fast to a first order stationary point. Using the same ideas that we presented in Section 5 this result implies that Projected Gradient Descent solves the LocalMin problem in time poly  $(1 / \varepsilon, L, G, d)$  when  $(\varepsilon, \delta)$  in the input are in the local regime. Also observe that although the following proof assumes access to the exact value of the gradient  $\nabla f$  it is very simple to adapt the proof to the case where we only have access to  $\nabla f$  with accuracy  $\varepsilon^3$ . We leave this as an exercise to the reader.

Theorem E.1. Let  $f: K \to \mathbb{R}$  be an L-smooth function and  $K \subseteq \mathbb{R}^d$  be a convex set. The projected gradient descent algorithm started at  $x_0$ , with step size  $\eta$ , after at most  $T \geq \frac{2L(f(x_0) - f(x^*))}{\varepsilon^2}$  steps outputs a point  $\hat{x}$  such that

$$
\| \hat {\boldsymbol {x}} - \Pi_ {K} (\hat {\boldsymbol {x}} - \eta \nabla f (\hat {\boldsymbol {x}})) \| _ {2} \leq \eta \cdot \varepsilon
$$

where  $\eta = 1 / L$  and  $x^{\star}$  is a global minimum of  $f$ .

Proof. If we run the Projected Gradient Descent algorithm on  $f$  then we have

$$
\boldsymbol {x} _ {t + 1} \leftarrow \Pi_ {K} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {t} - \eta \nabla f (\boldsymbol {x} _ {t})\right)
$$

then due to the  $L$ -smoothness of  $f$  we have that

$$
f (\boldsymbol {x} _ {t + 1}) \leq f (\boldsymbol {x} _ {t}) + \langle \nabla f (\boldsymbol {x} _ {t}), \boldsymbol {x} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {t} \rangle + \frac {L}{2} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

We can now apply Theorem 1.5.5 (b) of [FP07] to get that

$$
\langle \eta \cdot \nabla f (\boldsymbol {x} _ {t}), \boldsymbol {x} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {t} \rangle \leq - \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \Rightarrow
$$

$$
\langle \nabla f (\boldsymbol {x} _ {t}), \boldsymbol {x} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {t} \rangle \leq - \frac {1}{\eta} \cdot \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2}
$$

If we combine these then we have that

$$
f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {t + 1}\right) \leq f (\boldsymbol {x} _ {t}) - \left(\frac {1}{\eta} - \frac {L}{2}\right) \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

So if we pick  $\eta = 1 / L$  then we get

$$
f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {t + 1}\right) \leq f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {t}\right) - \frac {L}{2} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {t} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

If sum all the above inequalities and divide by  $T$  then we get

$$
\frac {1}{T} \sum_ {t = 0} ^ {T - 1} \| x _ {t + 1} - x _ {t} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq \frac {2}{T \cdot L} (f (x _ {0}) - f (x _ {T}))
$$

which implies that

$$
\min  _ {0 \leq t \leq T - 1} \| x _ {t + 1} - x _ {t} \| _ {2} \leq \sqrt {\frac {2}{T \cdot L} (f (x _ {0}) - f (x _ {T}))}
$$

Therefore for  $T \geq \frac{2L(f(x_0) - f(x^*))}{\varepsilon^2}$  we have that

$$
\min_{0\leq t\leq T - 1}\left\| \boldsymbol{x}_{t + 1} - \boldsymbol{x}_{t}\right\|_{2}\leq \eta \cdot \varepsilon = \varepsilon /L.
$$
